| *(didascalia)* | Enter old [[Queen Margaret]] |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | So now prosperity begins to mellow<br>And drop into the rotten mouth of [[Death\|death]].<br>Here in these confines slyly have I lurked<br>To watch the waning of mine enemies.<br>A dire induction am I witness to,<br>And will to France, hoping the consequence<br>Will prove as bitter, black, and tragical. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the old Duchess of York and [[Queen Elizabeth]] |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Withdraw thee, wretched Margaret. Who comes here? |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Ah, my poor princes! Ah, my tender babes!<br>My unblown [[Flowers\|flowers]], new-appearing sweets!<br>If yet your gentle souls fly in the air,<br>And be not fixed in doom perpetual,<br>Hover about me with your airy wings<br>And hear your mother’s lamentation. |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (aside)<br>Hover about her, say that right for right<br>Hath dimmed your infant morn to aged [[Night\|night]]. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | So many miseries have crazed my voice<br>That my woe-wearied tongue is still and mute.<br>Edward Plantagenet, why art thou dead? |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (aside)<br>Plantagenet doth quit Plantagenet;<br>Edward for Edward pays a dying debt. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Wilt thou, O God, fly from such gentle lambs<br>And throw them in the entrails of the wolf?<br>When didst thou [[Sleep\|sleep]], when such a deed was done? |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (aside)<br>When holy Harry died, and my sweet son. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Dead life, blind sight, poor mortal living [[Ghost\|ghost]],<br>Woe’s scene, world’s shame, [[Grave\|grave]]’s due by life<br>usurped,<br>Brief abstract and record of tedious days,<br>Rest thy unrest on England’s lawful earth,<br>Unlawfully made drunk with innocents’ [[Blood\|blood]].<br>They sit |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Ah that thou wouldst as soon afford a [[Grave\|grave]]<br>As thou canst yield a [[Melancholy\|melancholy]] seat.<br>Then would I hide my bones, not rest them here.<br>Ah, who hath any cause to mourn but we? |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | (coming forward)<br>If ancient sorrow be most reverend,<br>Give mine the benefit of seniory,<br>And let my griefs frown on the upper hand.<br>If sorrow can admit society,<br>Tell o’er your woes again by viewing mine.<br>I had an Edward, till a [[Richard]] killed him;<br>I had a husband, till a [[Richard]] killed him.<br>(To Elizabeth) Thou hadst an Edward, till a [[Richard]] killed him;<br>Thou hadst a [[Richard]], till a [[Richard]] killed him. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | rising<br>I had a [[Richard]] too, and thou didst kill him;<br>I had a Rutland too, thou holpst to kill him. |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Thou hadst a [[Clarence]] too, and [[Richard]] killed him.<br>From forth the kennel of thy womb hath crept<br>A hell-hound that doth hunt us all to [[Death\|death]]:<br>That dog that had his teeth before his eyes,<br>To worry lambs and lap their gentle [[Blood\|blood]];<br>That foul defacer of God’s handiwork,<br>That reigns in galled eyes of weeping souls;<br>That excellent grand [[Tyrant\|tyrant]] of the earth<br>Thy womb let loose to chase us to our graves.<br>O upright, just, and true-disposing God,<br>How do I thank thee that this charnel cur<br>Preys on the issue of his mother’s [[Body\|body]],<br>And makes her pewfellow with others’ moan. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | O Harry’s wife, triumph not in my woes.<br>God witness with me, I have wept for thine. |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Bear with me. I am hungry for [[Revenge\|revenge]],<br>And now I cloy me with beholding it.<br>Thy Edward, he is dead, that killed my Edward;<br>Thy other Edward dead, to quite my Edward;<br>Young York, he is but boot, because both they<br>Matched not the high perfection of my loss;<br>Thy [[Clarence]], he is dead, that stabbed my Edward,<br>And the beholders of this frantic plays—<br>Th’adulterate [[Hastings]], Rivers, Vaughan, Gray—<br>Untimely smothered in their dusky graves.<br>[[Richard]] yet lives, hell’s black intelligencer,<br>Only reserved their factor to buy souls<br>And send them thither; but at hand, at hand<br>Ensues his piteous and unpitied end.<br>Earth gapes, hell burns, fiends roar, saints pray,<br>To have him suddenly conveyed from hence.<br>Cancel his bond of life, dear God, I plead,<br>That I may live and say, ‘The dog is dead’. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | O thou didst prophesy the [[Time\|time]] would come<br>That I should wish for thee to help me curse<br>That bottled spider, that foul bunch-backed toad. |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | I called thee then ‘vain flourish of my fortune’;<br>I called thee then, poor [[Shadow\|shadow]], ‘painted [[Queen\|queen]]’—<br>The presentation of but what I was,<br>The flattering index of a direful pageant,<br>One heaved a-high to be hurled down below,<br>A mother only mocked with two fair babes,<br>[[A Dream\|A dream]] of what thou wast, a garish [[Flag\|flag]]<br>To be the aim of every dangerous shot,<br>A sign of dignity, a breath, a bubble,<br>A [[Queen\|queen]] in jest, only to fill the scene.<br>Where is thy husband now? Where be thy brothers?<br>Where are thy two sons? Wherein dost thou joy?<br>Who sues, and kneels, and says ‘God save the [[Queen]]’?<br>Where be the bending peers that flattered thee?<br>Where be the thronging troops that followed thee?<br>Decline all this, and see what now thou art:<br>For happy wife, a most distressed widow;<br>For joyful mother, one that wails the name;<br>For [[Queen\|queen]], a very caitiff, crowned with care;<br>For one being sued to, one that humbly sues;<br>For she that scorned at me, now scorned of me;<br>For she being feared of all, now fearing one;<br>For she commanding all, obeyed of none.<br>Thus hath the course of justice whirled about,<br>And left thee but a very prey to [[Time\|time]],<br>Having no more but thought of what thou wert<br>To torture thee the more, being what thou art.<br>Thou didst usurp my place, and dost thou not<br>Usurp the just proportion of my sorrow?<br>Now thy proud neck bears half my burdened yoke—<br>From which, even here, I slip my weary head,<br>And leave the burden of it all on thee.<br>Farewell, York’s wife, and [[Queen\|queen]] of sad mischance.<br>These English woes shall make me smile in France. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | (rising)<br>O thou, [[Well\|well]] skilled in curses, stay a while,<br>And teach me how to curse mine enemies. |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Forbear to [[Sleep\|sleep]] the nights, and fast the days;<br>Compare dead happiness with living woe;<br>Think that thy babes were sweeter than they were,<br>And he that slew them fouler than he is.<br>Bett’ring thy loss makes the bad causer worse.<br>Revolving this will teach thee how to curse. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | My words are dull. O quicken them with thine! |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | Thy woes will make them sharp and pierce like mine. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Why should calamity be full of words? |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Windy attorneys to their client woes,<br>Airy recorders of intestate joys,<br>Poor breathing orators of miseries.<br>Let them have scope. Though what they will impart<br>Help nothing else, yet do they ease the heart. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | If so, then be not tongue-tied; go with me,<br>And in the breath of bitter words let’s smother<br>My damned son, that thy two sweet sons smothered.<br>A march within<br>The trumpet sounds. Be copious in exclaims. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Richard]] and his train marching with drummers and trumpeters |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Who intercepts me in my expedition? |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | O, she that might have intercepted thee,<br>By strangling thee in her accursed womb,<br>From all the slaughters, wretch, that thou hast done. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Hid’st thou that forehead with a golden [[Crown\|crown]],<br>Where should be branded—if that right were right—<br>The slaughter of the prince that owed that [[Crown\|crown]],<br>And the dire [[Death\|death]] of my poor sons and brothers?<br>Tell me, thou [[Villain\|villain]]-slave, where are my children? |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Thou toad, thou toad, where is thy brother [[Clarence]]?<br>And little Ned Plantagenet his son? |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Where is the gentle Rivers, Vaughan, Gray? |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Where is kind [[Hastings]]? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | (to his train) |
| *(didascalia)* | A flourish, trumpets! Strike alarum, drums! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Let not the heavens hear these tell-tale women<br>Rail on the Lord’s anointed. Strike, I say! |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Alarums |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | (To the women) Either be patient and entreat me fair,<br>Or with the clamorous report of war<br>Thus will I drown your exclamations. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Art thou my son? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Ay, I thank God, my father, and yourself. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Then patiently hear my impatience. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Madam, I have a touch of your condition,<br>That cannot brook the accent of reproof. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | O let me speak! |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Do, then; but I’ll not hear. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | I will be mild and gentle in my words. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | And brief, good mother, for I am in haste. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Art thou so hasty? I have stayed for thee,<br>God knows, in torment and in agony— |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | And came I not at last to comfort you? |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | No, by the Holy Rood, thou know‘st it [[Well\|well]].<br>Thou cam’st on earth to make the earth my hell.<br>A grievous burden was thy birth to me;<br>Tetchy and wayward was thy infancy;<br>Thy schooldays frightful, desp’rate, wild, and furious;<br>Thy prime of manhood daring, bold, and venturous;<br>Thy age confirmed, proud, subtle, sly, and bloody;<br>More mild, but yet more harmful; kind in hatred.<br>What comfortable hour canst thou name<br>That ever graced me in thy company? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | [[Faith]], none but Humphrey Hewer, that called your grace<br>To breakfast once, forth of my company.<br>If I be so disgracious in your eye,<br>Let me march on, and not offend you, madam.—<br>Strike up the drum. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | I pray thee, hear me speak. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | You speak too bitterly. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Hear me a word,<br>For I shall never speak to thee again. |
| DUCHESS OF YORK | Either thou wilt die by God’s just ordinance<br>Ere from this war thou turn a conqueror,<br>Or I with grief and extreme age shall perish,<br>And never more behold thy face again.<br>Therefore take with thee my most heavy curse,<br>Which in the day of battle tire thee more<br>Than all the complete armour that thou wear’st.<br>My prayers on the adverse party fight,<br>And there the little souls of Edward’s children<br>Whisper the spirits of thine enemies,<br>And promise them [[success]] and victory.<br>Bloody thou art, bloody will be thy end;<br>Shame serves thy life, and doth thy [[Death\|death]] attend. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Though far more cause, yet much less spirit to curse<br>Abides in me; I say ‘Amen’ to all. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Stay, madam. I must talk a word with you. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | I have no more sons of the royal [[Blood\|blood]]<br>For thee to slaughter. For my daughters, [[Richard]],<br>They shall be praying nuns, not weeping queens,<br>And therefore level not to hit their lives. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | You have a daughter called Elizabeth,<br>Virtuous and fair, royal and gracious. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | And must she die for this? O let her live,<br>And I’ll corrupt her manners, stain her [[Beauty\|beauty]],<br>Slander myself as false to Edward’s bed,<br>Throw over her the veil of infamy.<br>So she may live unscarred of bleeding slaughter,<br>I will confess she was not Edward’s daughter. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Wrong not her birth. She is a royal princess. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | To save her life I’ll say she is not so. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Her life is safest only in her birth. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | And only in that safety died her brothers. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Lo, at their births good [[Stars\|stars]] were opposite. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | No, to their lives ill friends were contrary. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | All unavoided is the doom of destiny— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | True, when avoided grace makes destiny.<br>My babes were destined to a fairer [[Death\|death]],<br>If grace had blessed thee with a fairer life. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Madam, so thrive I in my enterprise<br>And dangerous [[success]] of bloody wars,<br>As I intend more good to you and yours<br>Than ever you or yours by me were harmed. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | What good is covered with the face of heaven,<br>To be discovered, that can do me good? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Th’advancement of your children, gentle lady. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Up to some scaffold, there to lose their heads. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Unto the dignity and height of fortune,<br>The high imperial type of this earth’s glory. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Flatter my sorrow with report of it.<br>Tell me what state, what dignity, what [[Honour\|honour]],<br>Canst thou demise to any [[Child\|child]] of mine? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Even all I have—ay, and myself and all,<br>Will I withal endow a [[Child\|child]] of thine,<br>So in the Lethe of thy angry [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Thou drown the sad remembrance of those wrongs,<br>Which thou supposest I have done to thee. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Be brief, lest that the process of thy kindness<br>Last longer telling than thy kindness’ date. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Then know that, from my [[Soul\|soul]], I [[Love\|love]] thy daughter. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | My daughter’s mother thinks that with her [[Soul\|soul]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | What do you think? |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | That thou dost [[Love\|love]] my daughter from thy [[Soul\|soul]];<br>So from thy [[Soul\|soul]]’s [[Love\|love]] didst thou [[Love\|love]] her brothers,<br>And from my heart’s [[Love\|love]] I do thank thee for it. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Be not so hasty to confound my meaning.<br>I mean, that with my [[Soul\|soul]] I [[Love\|love]] thy daughter,<br>And do intend to make her [[Queen\|queen]] of England. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | [[Well]] then, who dost thou mean shall be her [[King\|king]]? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Even he that makes her [[Queen\|queen]]. Who else should be? |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | What, thou? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Even so. How think you of it? |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | How canst thou woo her? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | That would I learn of you,<br>As one being best acquainted with her [[Humour\|humour]]. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | And wilt thou learn of me? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Madam, with all my heart. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Send to her, by the man that slew her brothers,<br>A pair of bleeding hearts; thereon engrave<br>‘Edward’ and ‘York’; then haply will she weep.<br>Therefore present to her—as sometimes Margaret<br>Did to thy father, steeped in Rutland’s [[Blood\|blood]]—<br>A handkerchief which, say to her, did drain<br>The purple sap from her sweet brother’s [[Body\|body]],<br>And bid her wipe her weeping eyes withal.<br>If this inducement move her not to [[Love\|love]],<br>Send her a letter of thy noble deeds.<br>Tell her thou mad’st away her uncle [[Clarence]],<br>Her uncle Rivers—ay, and for her sake<br>Mad’st quick conveyance with her good aunt Anne. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | You mock me, madam. This is not the way<br>To win your daughter. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | There is no other way,<br>Unless thou couldst put on some other shape,<br>And not be [[Richard]], that hath done all this. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Infer fair England’s peace by this alliance. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Which she shall purchase with still-lasting war. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Tell her the [[King]], that may command, entreats. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | That at her hands which the [[King]]’s [[King]] forbids. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Say she shall be a high and mighty [[Queen\|queen]]. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | To vail the title, as her mother doth. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Say I will [[Love\|love]] her everlastingly. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | But how long shall that title ‘ever’ last? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Sweetly in force unto her fair life’s end. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | But how long fairly shall her sweet life last? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | As long as heaven and [[Nature\|nature]] lengthens it. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | As long as hell and [[Richard]] likes of it. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Say I, her sovereign, am her subject [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | But she, your subject, loathes such sovereignty. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Be eloquent in my behalf to her. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | An honest tale speeds best being plainly told. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Then plainly to her tell my loving tale. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Plain and not honest is too harsh a style. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Your reasons are too shallow and too quick. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | O no, my reasons are too deep and dead—<br>Too deep and dead, poor infants, in their graves. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Harp not on that string, madam. That is past. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Harp on it still shall I, till heart-strings break. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Now by my George, my garter, and my [[Crown\|crown]]— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Profaned, dishonoured, and the third usurped. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | I swear— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | By nothing, for this is no oath.<br>Thy George, profaned, hath lost his holy [[Honour\|honour]];<br>Thy garter, blemished, pawned his lordly virtue;<br>Thy [[Crown\|crown]], usurped, disgraced his kingly glory.<br>If something thou wouldst swear to be believed,<br>Swear then by something that thou hast not wronged. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Then by mysetf— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Thy [[Self\|self]] is [[Self\|self]]-misused. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Now by the world— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | ‘Tis full of thy foul wrongs. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | My father’s [[Death\|death]]— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Thy life hath that dishonoured. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Why then, by God— |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | God’s wrong is most of all.<br>If thou didst [[Fear\|fear]] to break an oath with him,<br>The [[unity]] the [[King]] my husband made<br>Thou hadst not broken, nor my brothers died.<br>If thou hadst feared to break an oath by him,<br>Th’imperial metal circling now thy head<br>Had graced the tender temples of my [[Child\|child]],<br>And both the princes had been breathing here,<br>Which now—two tender bedfellows for [[Dust\|dust]]—<br>Thy broken [[Faith\|faith]] hath made the prey for worms.<br>What canst thou swear by now? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | The [[Time\|time]] to come. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | That thou hast wronged in the [[Time\|time]] o‘erpast,<br>For I myself have many [[Tears\|tears]] to wash<br>Hereafter [[Time\|time]], for [[Time\|time]] past wronged by thee.<br>The children live, whose fathers thou hast slaughtered—<br>Ungoverned youth, to wail it in their age.<br>The parents live, whose children thou hast butchered—<br>Old barren plants, to wail it with their age.<br>Swear not by [[Time\|time]] to come, for that thou hast<br>Misused ere used, by times ill-used o’erpast. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | As I intend to prosper and repent,<br>So thrive I in my dangerous affairs<br>Of hostile arms—myself myself confound,<br>Heaven and fortune bar me happy hours,<br>Day yield me not thy light nor [[Night\|night]] thy rest;<br>Be opposite, all planets of good luck,<br>To my proceeding—if, with dear heart’s [[Love\|love]],<br>Immaculate devotion, holy thoughts,<br>I tender not thy beauteous, princely daughter.<br>In her consists my happiness and thine.<br>Without her follows—to myself and thee,<br>Herself, the land, and many a Christian [[Soul\|soul]]—<br>[[Death]], desolation, ruin, and [[Decay\|decay]].<br>It cannot be avoided but by this;<br>It will not be avoided but by this.<br>Therefore, good-mother—I must call you so—<br>Be the attorney of my [[Love\|love]] to her.<br>Plead what I will be, not what I have been;<br>Not my deserts, but what I will deserve.<br>Urge the necessity and state of times,<br>And be not peevish-fond in great designs. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Shall I be tempted of the devil thus? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Ay, if the devil tempt you to do good. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Shall I forget myself to be myself? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Ay, if yourself’s remembrance wrong yourself. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Yet thou didst kill my children. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | But in your daughter’s womb I bury them,<br>Where, in that nest of spicery, they will breed<br>Selves of themselves, to your recomfiture. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Shall I go win my daughter to thy will? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | And be a happy mother by the deed. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | I go. Write to me very shortly,<br>And you shall understand from me her mind. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Bear her my true [[Love\|love]]’s kiss,<br>He kisses her<br>and so farewell— |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Elizabeth |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Relenting [[Fool\|fool]], and shallow, changing woman. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Sir [[Richard]] Ratcliffe |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | How now, what news? |
| RATCLIFFE | Most mighty sovereign, on the western coast<br>Rideth a puissant navy. To our shores<br>Throng many doubtful, hollow-hearted friends,<br>Unarmed and unresolved, to beat them back.<br>‘Tis thought that Richmond is their admiral,<br>And there they hull, expecting but the aid<br>Of [[Buckingham]] to welcome them ashore. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Some light-foot friend post to the Duke of Norfolk.<br>Ratcliffe thyself, or Catesby—where is he? |
| CATESBY | Here, my good lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Catesby, fly to the Duke. |
| CATESBY | I will, my lord, with all convenient haste. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Ratcliffe, come hither. Post to Salisbury;<br>When thou com‘st thither—(to Catesby) dull, unmindful [[Villain\|villain]],<br>Why stay’st thou here, and goest not to the Duke? |
| CATESBY | First, mighty liege, tell me your highness’ pleasure:<br>What from your grace I shall deliver to him? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | O true, good Catesby. Bid him levy straight<br>The greatest strength and power that he can make,<br>And meet me suddenly at Salisbury. |
| CATESBY | I go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| RATCLIFFE | What, may it please you, shall I do at Salisbury? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Why, what wouldst thou do there before I go? |
| RATCLIFFE | Your highness told me I should post before. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | My mind is changed. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord Stanley |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Stanley, what news with you? |
| STANLEY | None, good my liege, to please you with the hearing,<br>Nor none so bad but [[Well\|well]] may be reported. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Hoyday, a [[Riddle\|riddle]]! Neither good nor bad.<br>Why need’st thou run so many mile about<br>When thou mayst tell thy tale the nearest way?<br>Once more, what news? |
| STANLEY | Richmond is on the seas. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | There let him sink, and be the seas on him.<br>White-livered renegade, what doth he there? |
| STANLEY | I know not, mighty sovereign, but by guess. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | [[Well]], as you guess? |
| STANLEY | Stirred up by Dorset, [[Buckingham]], and Ely,<br>He makes for England, here to claim the [[Crown\|crown]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Is the chair empty? Is the [[Sword\|sword]] unswayed?<br>Is the [[King]] dead? The empire unpossessed?<br>What heir of York is there alive but we?<br>And who is England’s [[King\|king]] but great York’s heir?<br>Then tell me, what makes he upon the seas? |
| STANLEY | Unless for that, my liege, I cannot guess. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Unless for that he comes to be your liege,<br>You cannot guess wherefore the Welshman comes.<br>Thou wilt revolt and fly to him, I [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| STANLEY | No, my good lord, therefore mistrust me not. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Where is thy power then? To beat him back,<br>Where be thy tenants and thy followers?<br>Are they not now upon the western shore,<br>Safe-conducting the rebels from their ships? |
| STANLEY | No, my good lord, my friends are in the north. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Cold friends to me. What do they in the north,<br>When they should serve their sovereign in the west? |
| STANLEY | They have not been commanded, mighty [[King]].<br>Pleaseth your majesty to give me leave,<br>I’ll muster up my friends and meet your grace<br>Where and what [[Time\|time]] your majesty shall please. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Ay, ay, thou wouldst be gone to join with Richmond.<br>But I’ll not trust thee. |
| STANLEY | Most mighty sovereign,<br>You have no cause to hold my [[Friendship\|friendship]] doubtful.<br>I never was, nor never will be, false. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Go then and muster men—but leave behind<br>Your son George Stanley. Look your heart be firm,<br>Or else his head’s assurance is but frail. |
| STANLEY | So deal with him as I prove true to you. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | My gracious sovereign, now in Devonshire,<br>As I by friends am [[Well\|well]] advertised,<br>Sir Edward Courtenay and the haughty prelate,<br>Bishop of Exeter, his elder brother,<br>With many more confederates are in arms. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter another Messenger |
| SECOND MESSENGER | In [[Kent]], my liege, the Guildfords are in arms,<br>And every hour more competitors<br>Flock to the rebels, and their power grows strong. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter another Messenger |
| THIRD MESSENGER | My lord, the army of great [[Buckingham]]— |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Out on ye, owls! Nothing but [[songs]] of [[Death\|death]]?<br>He striketh him<br>There, take thou that, till thou bring better news. |
| THIRD MESSENGER | The news I have to tell your majesty<br>Is that, by sudden flood and fall of [[Water\|water]],<br>[[Buckingham]]’s army is dispersed and scattered,<br>And he himself wandered away [[Alone\|alone]],<br>No man knows whither. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | I cry thee [[Mercy\|mercy]].—<br>Ratcliffe, reward him for the blow I gave him.—<br>Hath any [[Well\|well]]-advisèd friend proclaimed<br>Reward to him that brings the traitor in? |
| THIRD MESSENGER | Such proclamation hath been made, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter another Messenger |
| FOURTH MESSENGER | Sir Thomas Lovell and Lord Marquis Dorset—<br>‘Tis said, my liege—in Yorkshire are in arms.<br>But this good comfort bring I to your highness:<br>The Breton navy is dispersed by tempest.<br>Richmond in Dorsetshire sent out a boat<br>Unto the shore, to ask those on the banks<br>If they were his assistants, yea or no?<br>Who answered him they came from [[Buckingham]]<br>Upon his party. He, mistrusting them,<br>Hoist sail and made his course again for Bretagne. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | March on, march on, since we are up in arms,<br>If not to fight with foreign enemies,<br>Yet to beat down these rebels here at [[Home\|home]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Catesby |
| CATESBY | My liege, the [[Duke of Buckingham]] is taken.<br>That is the best news. That the [[Earl of Richmond]]<br>Is with a mighty power landed at Milford<br>Is colder tidings, yet they must be told. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Away, towards Salisbury! While we reason here,<br>A royal battle might be won and lost.<br>Someone take order [[Buckingham]] be brought<br>To Salisbury. The rest march on with me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord Stanley Earl of Derby and Sir Christopher, a priest |
| STANLEY | Sir Christopher, tell Richmond this from me:<br>That in the sty of this most deadly boar<br>My son George Stanley is franked up in hold.<br>If I revolt, off goes young George’s head.<br>The [[Fear\|fear]] of that holds off my present aid.<br>But tell me, where is princely Richmond now? |
| SIR CHRISTOPHER | At Pembroke, or at Ha’rfordwest in Wales. |
| STANLEY | What men of name resort to him? |
| SIR CHRISTOPHER | Sir Walter Herbert, a renowned soldier,<br>Sir Gilbert Talbot, Sir William Stanley,<br>[[Oxford]], redoubted Pembroke, Sir James Blunt,<br>And Rhys-ap-Thomas with a valiant crew,<br>And many other of great name and worth—<br>And towards [[London]] do they bend their power,<br>If by the way they be not fought withal. |
| STANLEY | [[Well]], hie thee to thy lord. Commend me to him.<br>Tell him the [[Queen]] hath heartily consented<br>He should espouse Elizabeth her daughter.<br>My letter will resolve him of my mind.<br>Farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 5, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Buckingham]] with halberdiers, led by a Sheriff to execution |
| [[Buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Will not [[King]] [[Richard]] let me speak with him? |
| SHERIFF | No, my good lord, therefore be patient. |
| [[Buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | [[Hastings]], and Edward’s children, Gray and Rivers,<br>Holy [[King]] Henry and thy fair son Edward,<br>Vaughan, and all that have miscarried<br>By underhand, corrupted, foul injustice:<br>If that your moody, discontented souls<br>Do through the clouds behold this present hour,<br>Even for [[Revenge\|revenge]] mock my destruction.<br>This is All-Souls’ day, fellow, is it not? |
| SHERIFF | It is. |
| [[Buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Why then All-Souls’ day is my [[Body\|body]]’s doomsday.<br>This is the day which, in [[King]] Edward’s [[Time\|time]],<br>I wished might fall on me, when I was found<br>False to his children and his wife’s allies.<br>This is the day wherein I wished to fall<br>By the false [[Faith\|faith]] of him whom most I trusted.<br>This, this All-Souls’ day to my fearful [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Is the determined respite of my wrongs.<br>That high all-seer which I dallied with<br>Hath turned my feigned [[prayer]] on my head,<br>And given in earnest what I begged in jest.<br>Thus doth he force the swords of wicked men<br>To turn their own points in their masters’ bosoms.<br>Thus Margaret’s curse falls heavy on my neck.<br>‘When he’, quoth she, ‘shall split thy heart with sorrow,<br>Remember Margaret was a prophetess.’<br>Come lead me, officers, to the block of shame.<br>Wrong hath but wrong, and blame the due of blame. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Henry [[Earl of Richmond]] with a letter, the Earl of [[Oxford]], Sir James Blunt, Sir Walter Herbert, and others, with drum and colours |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | Fellows in arms, and my most loving friends,<br>Bruised underneath the yoke of tyranny,<br>Thus far into the bowels of the land<br>Have we marched on without impediment,<br>And here receive we from our father Stanley<br>Lines of fair comfort and encouragement.<br>The wretched, bloody, and usurping boar,<br>That spoils your [[Summer\|summer]] fields and fruitful vines,<br>Swills your warm [[Blood\|blood]] like wash, and makes his trough<br>In your inbowelled bosoms, this foul swine<br>Lies now even in the centry of this isle,<br>Near to the town of Leicester, as we learn.<br>From Tamworth thither is but one day’s march.<br>In God’s name, cheerly on, courageous friends,<br>To reap the harvest of perpetual peace<br>By this one bloody trial of sharp war. |
| [[Oxford\|OXFORD]] | Every man’s [[Conscience\|conscience]] is a thousand swords<br>To fight against this guilty homicide. |
| HERBERT | I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not but his friends will turn to us. |
| BLUNT | He hath no friends but what are friends for [[Fear\|fear]],<br>Which in his dearest need will fly from him. |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | All for our vantage. Then, in God’s name, march.<br>True [[Hope\|hope]] is swift, and flies with swallows’ wings;<br>Kings it makes gods, and meaner creatures kings. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt marching |

### Atto 5, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Richard]] in arms, with the Duke of Norfolk, Sir [[Richard]] Ratcliffe, Sir William Catesby, and others |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Here pitch our tent, even here in Bosworth field. |
| *(didascalia)* | Soldiers begin to pitch a tent |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Why, how now, Catesby? Why look you so sad? |
| *(didascalia)* | CATESBY |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | My heart is ten times lighter than my looks. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | My lord of Norfolk. |
| NORFOLK | Here, most gracious liege. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Norfolk, we must have knocks. Ha, must we not? |
| NORFOLK | We must both give and take, my loving lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Up with my tent! Here will I lie tonight.<br>But where tomorrow? [[Well]], all’s one for that.<br>Who hath descried the number of the traitors? |
| NORFOLK | Six or seven thousand is their utmost power. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Why, our battalia trebles that account.<br>Besides, the [[King]]’s name is a tower of strength,<br>Which they upon the adverse faction want.<br>Up with the tent! Come, noble gentlemen,<br>Let us survey the vantage of the ground.<br>Call for some men of sound direction.<br>Let’s lack no discipline, make no delay—<br>For, lords, tomorrow is a busy day. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt at one door |

### Atto 5, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter at another door Henry [[Earl of Richmond]], Sir James Blunt, Sir William Brandon, the Earl of [[Oxford]], Marquis Dorset, and others |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | The weary sun hath made a golden set,<br>And by the bright track of his fiery car<br>Gives token of a goodly day tomorrow.<br>Sir William Brandon, you shall bear my standard.<br>The Earl of Pembroke keeps his regiment;<br>Good Captain Blunt, bear my good [[Night\|night]] to him,<br>And by the second hour in the morning<br>[[Desire]] the Earl to see me in my tent.<br>Yet one thing more, good Captain, do for me:<br>Where is Lord Stanley quartered, do you know? |
| BLUNT | Unless I have mista’en his colours much,<br>Which [[Well\|well]] I am assured I have not done,<br>His regiment lies half a mile, at least,<br>South from the mighty power of the [[King]]. |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | If without peril it be possible,<br>Sweet Blunt, make some good means to speak with him,<br>And give him from me this most needful note. |
| BLUNT | Upon my life, my lord, I’ll undertake it.<br>And so God give you quiet rest tonight. |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | Good [[Night\|night]], good Captain Blunt. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Blunt |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | Come, gentlemen.<br>Give me some ink and paper in my tent.<br>I’ll draw the form and model of our battle,<br>Limit each leader to his several charge,<br>And part in just proportion our small power.<br>Let us consult upon tomorrow’s business.<br>Into my tent: the dew is raw and cold.<br>They withdraw into the tent |

### Atto 5, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | A table brought in. Enter [[King]] [[Richard]], Sir [[Richard]] Ratcliffe, the Duke of Norfolk, Sir William Catesby, and others |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | What is’t o’[[Clock\|clock]]? |
| CATESBY | It’s supper-[[Time\|time]], my lord. It’s nine o’[[Clock\|clock]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | I will not sup tonight. Give me some ink and paper.<br>What, is my beaver easier than it was?<br>And all my armour laid into my tent? |
| CATESBY | It is, my liege, and all things are in readiness. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Good Norfolk, hie thee to thy charge.<br>Use careful watch; choose trusty sentinels. |
| NORFOLK | I go, my lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Stir with the lark tomorrow, gentle Norfolk. |
| NORFOLK | I warrant you, my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Catesby. |
| CATESBY | My lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Send out a pursuivant-at-arms<br>To Stanley’s regiment. Bid him bring his power<br>Before sun-rising, lest his son George fall<br>Into the blind [[Cave\|cave]] of eternal [[Night\|night]].<br>Fill me a bowl of wine. Give me a watch.<br>Saddle white Surrey for the field tomorrow.<br>Look that my staves be sound, and not too heavy.<br>Ratcliffe. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Catesby |
| RATCLIFFE | My lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Saw’st thou the [[Melancholy\|melancholy]] Lord Northumberland? |
| RATCLIFFE | Thomas the [[Earl of Surrey]] and himself,<br>Much about cockshut [[Time\|time]], from troop to troop<br>Went through the army, cheering up the soldiers. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | So, I am satisfied. Give me some wine.<br>I have not that alacrity of spirit,<br>Nor cheer of mind, that I was wont to have.<br>The wine is brought<br>Set it down. Is ink and paper ready? |
| RATCLIFFE | It is, my lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Leave me. Bid my guard watch.<br>About the mid of [[Night\|night]] come to my tent,<br>Ratcliffe, and help to arm me. Leave me, I say. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Ratcliffe with others. [[Richard]] writes, and later sleeps |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord Stanley Earl of Derby to Henry [[Earl of Richmond]] and the lords in his tent |
| STANLEY | Fortune and victory sit on thy helm! |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | All comfort that the dark [[Night\|night]] can afford<br>Be to thy person, noble father-in-law.<br>Tell me, how fares our loving mother? |
| STANLEY | I, by attorney, bless thee from thy mother,<br>Who prays continually for Richmond’s good.<br>So much for that. The silent hours steal on,<br>And flaky darkness breaks within the east.<br>In brief—for so the season bids us be—<br>Prepare thy battle early in the morning,<br>And put thy fortune to th’arbitrement<br>Of bloody strokes and mortal-sharing war.<br>I, as I may—that which I would, I cannot—<br>With best advantage will deceive the [[Time\|time]],<br>And aid thee in this doubtful shock of arms.<br>But on thy side I may not be too forward—<br>Lest, being seen, thy brother, tender George,<br>Be executed in his father’s sight.<br>Farewell. The leisure and the fearful [[Time\|time]]<br>Cuts off the ceremonious vows of [[Love\|love]]<br>And ample interchange of sweet discourse,<br>Which so long sundered friends should dwell upon.<br>God give us leisure for these rights of [[Love\|love]].<br>Once more, adieu. Be valiant, and [[Speed\|speed]] [[Well\|well]]. |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | Good lords, conduct him to his regiment.<br>I’ll strive with troubled thoughts to take a nap,<br>Lest leaden slumber peise me down tomorrow,<br>When I should mount with wings of victory.<br>Once more, good [[Night\|night]], kind lords and gentlemen. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Stanley and the lords |
| *(didascalia)* | Richmond kneels |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | O thou, whose captain I account myself,<br>Look on my forces with a gracious eye.<br>Put in their hands thy bruising irons of wrath,<br>That they may crush down with a heavy fall<br>Th’usurping helmets of our adversaries.<br>Make us thy ministers of chastisement,<br>That we may praise thee in the victory.<br>To thee I do commend my watchful [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Ere I let fall the windows of mine eyes.<br>Sleeping and waking, O defend me still! He sleeps |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Ghost]] of young Prince Edward above |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF PRINCE EDWARD | (to [[Richard]])<br>Let me sit heavy on thy [[Soul\|soul]] tomorrow,<br>Prince Edward, son to Henry the Sixth.<br>Think how thou stabbedst me in my prime of youth<br>At Tewkesbury. [[Despair]], therefore, and die.<br>(To Richmond) Be cheerful, Richmond, for the wronged<br>souls<br>Of butchered princes fight in thy behalf.<br>[[King]] Henry’s issue, Richmond, comforts thee. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghost]] of [[King]] Henry the Sixth |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF [[King\|KING]] HENRY | (to [[Richard]])<br>When I was mortal, my anointed [[Body\|body]]<br>By thee was punched full of deadly holes.<br>Think on the Tower and me. [[Despair]] and die.<br>Harry the Sixth bids thee [[Despair\|despair]] and die.<br>(To Richmond) Virtuous and holy, be thou conqueror.<br>Harry that prophesied thou shouldst be [[King\|king]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Comforts thee in thy [[Sleep\|sleep]]. Live and flourish! Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghost]] of George Duke of [[Clarence]] |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | (to [[Richard]])<br>Let me sit heavy on thy [[Soul\|soul]] tomorrow,<br>I that was washed to [[Death\|death]] with fulsome wine,<br>Poor [[Clarence]], by thy guile betrayed to [[Death\|death]].<br>Tomorrow in the battle think on me,<br>And fall thy edgeless [[Sword\|sword]]. [[Despair]] and die.<br>(To Richmond) Thou offspring of the [[house]] of<br>Lancaster,<br>The wronged heirs of York do pray for thee.<br>Good angels guard thy battle. Live and flourish! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghosts]] of Lord Rivers, Lord Gray, and Sir Thomas Vaughan |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF RIVERS | (to [[Richard]])<br>Let me sit heavy on thy [[Soul\|soul]] tomorrow,<br>Rivers that died at Pomfret. [[Despair]] and die. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF GRAY | (to [[Richard]])<br>Think upon Gray, and let thy [[Soul\|soul]] [[Despair\|despair]]. |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF VAUGHAN | (to [[Richard]])<br>Think upon Vaughan, and with guilty [[Fear\|fear]]<br>Let fall thy pointless lance. [[Despair]] and die. |
| ALL THREE | (to Richmond)<br>Awake, and think our wrongs in [[Richard]]’s bosom<br>Will conquer him. Awake, and win the day! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Ghosts]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghosts]] of the two young Princes |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Ghosts\|GHOSTS]] OF THE PRINCES (to [[Richard]]) |
| ALL THREE | Dream on thy cousins, smothered in the Tower.<br>Let us be lead within thy bosom, [[Richard]],<br>And weigh thee down to ruin, shame, and [[Death\|death]].<br>Thy nephews’ souls bid thee [[Despair\|despair]] and die.<br>(To Richmond) [[Sleep]], Richmond, [[Sleep\|sleep]] in peace and wake in joy.<br>Good angels guard thee from the boar’s annoy.<br>Live, and beget a happy race of kings!<br>Edward’s unhappy sons do bid thee flourish. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Ghosts]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghost]] of Lord [[Hastings]] |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | (to [[Richard]])<br>Bloody and guilty, guiltily awake,<br>And in a bloody battle end thy days.<br>Think on Lord [[Hastings]], then [[Despair\|despair]] and die.<br>(To Richmond) Quiet, untroubled [[Soul\|soul]], awake, awake!<br>Arm, fight, and conquer for fair England’s sake. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghost]] of [[Lady Anne]] |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF [[Lady Anne\|LADY ANNE]] | (to [[Richard]])<br>[[Richard]], thy wife, that wretched Anne thy wife,<br>That never slept a quiet hour with thee,<br>Now fills thy [[Sleep\|sleep]] with perturbations.<br>Tomorrow in the battle think on me,<br>And fall thy edgeless [[Sword\|sword]]. [[Despair]] and die.<br>(To Richmond) Thou quiet [[Soul\|soul]], [[Sleep\|sleep]] thou a quiet [[Sleep\|sleep]].<br>Dream of [[success]] and happy victory. |
| *(didascalia)* | Thy adversary’s wife doth pray for thee. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter above the [[Ghost]] of the [[Duke of Buckingham]] |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF [[Buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | (to [[Richard]])<br>The first was I that helped thee to the [[Crown\|crown]];<br>The last was I that felt thy tyranny.<br>O in the battle think on [[Buckingham]],<br>And die in terror of thy guiltiness!<br>Dream on, dream on, of bloody deeds and [[Death\|death]];<br>Fainting, [[Despair\|despair]]; despairing, yield thy breath.<br>(To Richmond) I died for [[Hope\|hope]] ere I could lend thee aid.<br>But cheer thy heart, and be thou not dismayed.<br>God and good angels fight on Richmond’s side, |
| *(didascalia)* | And [[Richard]] falls in height of all his [[Pride\|pride]]. Exit |
| [[Ghost\|GHOST]] OF [[Buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | [[Richard]] starteth up out of [[A Dream\|a dream]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Give me another [[Horse\|horse]]! Bind up my wounds!<br>Have [[Mercy\|mercy]], Jesu!—Soft, I did but dream.<br>O coward [[Conscience\|conscience]], how dost thou afflict me?<br>The lights burn blue. It is now dead midnight.<br>Cold fearful drops stand on my trembling flesh.<br>What do I [[Fear]]? Myself? There’s none else by.<br>[[Richard]] loves [[Richard]]; that is, I am I.<br>Is there a murderer here? No. Yes, I am.<br>Then fly! What, from myself? Great reason. Why?<br>Lest I [[Revenge\|revenge]]. Myself upon myself?<br>Alack, I [[Love\|love]] myself. Wherefore? For any good<br>That I myself have done unto myself?<br>O no, alas, I rather hate myself<br>For hateful deeds committed by myself.<br>I am a [[Villain\|villain]]. Yet I lie: I am not.<br>[[Fool]], of thyself speak [[Well\|well]].—[[Fool]], do not flatter.<br>My [[Conscience\|conscience]] hath a thousand several tongues,<br>And every tongue brings in a several tale,<br>And every tale condemns me for a [[Villain\|villain]].<br>Perjury, perjury, in the high‘st degree!<br>Murder, stern murder, in the dir’st degree!<br>All several sins, all used in each degree,<br>Throng to the bar, crying all, ‘Guilty, guilty!’<br>I shall [[Despair\|despair]]. There is no creature loves me,<br>And if I die no [[Soul\|soul]] will pity me.<br>Nay, wherefore should they?—Since that I myself<br>Find in myself no pity to myself.<br>Methought the souls of all that I had murdered<br>Came to my tent, and every one did threat<br>Tomorrow’s vengeance on the head of [[Richard]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Ratcliffe |
| RATCLIFFE | My lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | ‘Swounds, who is there? |
| RATCLIFFE | My lord, ’tis I. The early village cock<br>Hath twice done salutation to the morn.<br>Your friends are up, and buckle on their armour. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | O Ratcliffe, I have dreamed a fearful dream.<br>What thinkest thou, will all our friends prove true? |
| RATCLIFFE | No [[Doubt\|doubt]], my lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Ratcliffe, I [[Fear\|fear]], I [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| RATCLIFFE | Nay, good my lord, be not afraid of shadows. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | By the Apostle Paul, shadows tonight<br>Have struck more terror to the [[Soul\|soul]] of [[Richard]]<br>Than can the substance of ten thousand soldiers<br>Armed in proof and led by shallow Richmond.<br>’Tis not yet near day. Come, go with me.<br>Under our tents I’ll play the eavesdropper,<br>To see if any mean to shrink from me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Richard]] and Ratcliffe |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the lords to Henry [[Earl of Richmond]], sitting in his tent |
| *(didascalia)* | LORDS Good morrow, Richmond. |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | Cry [[Mercy\|mercy]], lords and watchful gentlemen,<br>That you have ta’en a tardy sluggard here. |
| *(didascalia)* | A LORDHow have you slept, my lord? |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | The sweetest [[Sleep\|sleep]] and fairest boding [[Dreams\|dreams]]<br>That ever entered in a drowsy head<br>Have I since your departure had, my lords.<br>Methought their souls whose bodies [[Richard]] murdered<br>Came to my tent and cried on victory.<br>I promise you, my [[Soul\|soul]] is very jocund<br>In the remembrance of so fair [[A Dream\|a dream]].<br>How far into the morning is it, lords?<br>A LORD Upon the stroke of four. |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | Why then, ’tis [[Time\|time]] to arm, and give direction.<br>His oration to his soldiers<br>Much that I could say, loving countrymen,<br>The leisure and enforcement of the [[Time\|time]]<br>Forbids to dwell on. Yet remember this:<br>God and our good cause fight upon our side.<br>The prayers of holy saints and wrongèd souls,<br>Like high-reared bulwarks, stand before our forces.<br>[[Richard]] except, those whom we fight against<br>Had rather have us win than him they follow.<br>For what is he they follow? Truly, friends,<br>A bloody [[Tyrant\|tyrant]] and a homicide;<br>One raised in [[Blood\|blood]], and one in [[Blood\|blood]] established;<br>One that made means to come by what he hath,<br>And slaughtered those that were the means to help him;<br>A base, foul stone, made precious by the foil<br>Of England’s chair, where he is falsely set;<br>One that hath ever been God’s enemy.<br>Then if you fight against God’s enemy,<br>God will, in justice, ward you as his soldiers.<br>If you do sweat to put a [[Tyrant\|tyrant]] down,<br>You [[Sleep\|sleep]] in peace, the [[Tyrant\|tyrant]] being slain.<br>If you do fight against your country’s foes,<br>Your country’s foison pays your pains the hire.<br>If you do fight in safeguard of your wives,<br>Your wives shall welcome [[Home\|home]] the conquerors.<br>If you do free your children from the [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Your children’s children quites it in your age.<br>Then, in the name of God and all these rights,<br>Advance your standards! Draw your willing swords!<br>For me, the ransom of this bold attempt<br>Shall be my cold corpse on the earth’s cold face;<br>But if I thrive, to gain of my attempt,<br>The least of you shall share his part thereof. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound, drums and trumpets, bold and cheerfully! |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | God and Saint George! Richmond and victory! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt to the sound of drums and trumpets |

### Atto 5, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King]] [[Richard]], Sir [[Richard]] Ratcliffe, Sir William Catesby, and others |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | What said Northumberland, as touching Richmond? |
| RATCLIFFE | That he was never trained up in arms. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | He said the [[Truth\|truth]]. And what said Surrey then? |
| RATCLIFFE | He smiled and said, ‘The better for our purpose.’ |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | He was in the right, and so indeed it is.<br>[[Clock]] strikes<br>Tell the [[Clock\|clock]] there. Give me a calendar.<br>Who saw the sun today? |
| *(didascalia)* | A book is brought |
| RATCLIFFE | Not I, my lord. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Then he disdains to shine, for by the book<br>He should have braved the east an hour ago.<br>A black day will it be to somebody.<br>Ratcliffe. |
| RATCLIFFE | My lord? |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | The sun will not be seen today.<br>The sky doth frown and lour upon our army.<br>I would these dewy [[Tears\|tears]] were from the ground.<br>Not shine today—why, what is that to me<br>More than to Richmond? For the selfsame heaven<br>That frowns on me looks sadly upon him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Norfolk |
| NORFOLK | Arm, arm, my lord! The foe vaunts in the field. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Come, bustle, bustle! Caparison my [[Horse\|horse]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Richard]] arms |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Call up Lord Stanley, bid him bring his power.<br>I will lead forth my soldiers to the plain,<br>And thus my battle shall be orderèd.<br>My forward shall be drawn out all in length,<br>Consisting equally of [[Horse\|horse]] add foot,<br>Our archers placèd strongly in the midst.<br>John Duke of Norfolk, Thomas [[Earl of Surrey]],<br>Shall have the leading of this multitude.<br>They thus directed, we ourself will follow<br>In the main battle, whose puissance on both sides<br>Shall be [[Well\|well]] wingèd with our chiefest [[Horse\|horse]].<br>This, and Saint George to boot! What think’st thou, Norfolk? |
| NORFOLK | A good direction, warlike sovereign.<br>He showeth him a paper<br>This paper found I on my tent this morning.<br>(He reads)<br>‘Jackie of Norfolk be not too bold,<br>For Dickon thy master is bought and sold.’ |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | A thing devisèd by the enemy.—<br>Go, gentlemen, each man unto his charge.<br>Let not our babbling [[Dreams\|dreams]] affright our souls.<br>[[Conscience]] is but a word that cowards use,<br>Devised at first to keep the strong in awe.<br>Our strong arms be our [[Conscience\|conscience]]; swords, our law.<br>March on, join bravely! Let us to’t, pell mell—<br>If not to heaven, then hand in hand to hell.<br>His oration to his army<br>What shall I say, more than I have inferred?<br>Remember whom you are to cope withal:<br>A sort of vagabonds, rascals and runaways,<br>A scum of Bretons and base lackey peasants,<br>Whom their o‘ercloyèd country vomits forth<br>To desperate ventures and assured destruction.<br>You sleeping safe, they bring to you unrest;<br>You having lands and blessed with beauteous wives,<br>They would distrain the one, distain the other.<br>And who doth lead them, but a paltry fellow?<br>Long kept in Bretagne at our mother’s cost;<br>A milksop; one that never in his life<br>Felt so much cold as over shoes in [[Snow\|snow]].<br>Let’s whip these stragglers o’er the seas again,<br>Lash hence these overweening rags of France,<br>These famished beggars, weary of their lives,<br>Who—but for dreaming on this fond exploit—<br>For want of means, poor rats, had hanged themselves.<br>If we be conquered, let men conquer us,<br>And not these bastard Bretons, whom our fathers<br>Have in their own land beaten, bobbed, and thumped,<br>And in record left them the heirs of shame.<br>Shall these enjoy our lands? Lie with our wives?<br>Ravish our daughters? |
| *(didascalia)* | Drum afar off |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Hark, I hear their drum.<br>Fight, gentlemen of England! Fight, bold yeomen!<br>Draw, archers, draw your arrows to the head!<br>Spur your proud horses hard, and ride in [[Blood\|blood]]!<br>Amaze the welkin with your broken staves! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | What says Lord Stanley? Will he bring his power? |
| MESSENGER | My lord, he doth deny to come. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Off with young George’s head! |
| NORFOLK | My lord, the enemy is past the marsh.<br>After the battle let George Stanley die. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | A thousand hearts are great within my bosom.<br>Advance our standards! Set upon our foes!<br>Our ancient word of [[Courage\|courage]], fair Saint George,<br>Inspire us with the spleen of fiery dragons.<br>Upon them! Victory sits on our helms! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 7

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum. Excursions. Enter Sir William Catesby |
| CATESBY | calling<br>Rescue, my lord of Norfolk! Rescue, rescue! |
| *(didascalia)* | To a soldier The [[King]] enacts more wonders than a man, |
| CATESBY | Daring an opposite to every danger.<br>His [[Horse\|horse]] is slain, and all on foot he fights,<br>Seeking for Richmond in the throat of [[Death\|death]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Calling Rescue, fair lord, or else the day is lost! |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarums. Enter [[King]] [[Richard]] |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | A [[Horse\|horse]]! A [[Horse\|horse]]! My kingdom for a [[Horse\|horse]]! |
| CATESBY | Withdraw, my lord. I’ll help you to a [[Horse\|horse]]. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Slave, I have set my life upon a cast,<br>And I will stand the hazard of the die.<br>I think there be six Richmonds in the field.<br>Five have I slain today, instead of him.<br>A [[Horse\|horse]]! A [[Horse\|horse]]! My kingdom for a [[Horse\|horse]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 8

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum. Enter [[King]] [[Richard]] |
| *(didascalia)* | at one door and Henry [[Earl of Richmond]] |
| *(didascalia)* | at another. They fight. [[Richard]] is slain. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Richmond. |
| *(didascalia)* | Retreat and flourish. Enter Henry [[Earl of Richmond]] and Lord Stanley Earl of Derby, with divers other lords and soldiers |
| HENRY [[Earl of Richmond\|EARL OF RICHMOND]] | God and your arms be praised, victorious friends!<br>The day is ours. The bloody dog is dead. |
| STANLEY | (bearing the [[Crown\|crown]])<br>Courageous Richmond, [[Well\|well]] hast thou acquit thee.<br>Lo, here this long usurpèd royalty<br>From the dead temples of this bloody wretch<br>Have I plucked off, to grace thy brows withal.<br>Wear it, enjoy it, and make much of it. |
| *(didascalia)* | He sets the [[Crown\|crown]] on Henry’s head |
| [[King\|KING]] HENRY THE SEVENTH | Great God of heaven, say ‘Amen’ to all.<br>But tell me—young George Stanley, is he living? |
| STANLEY | He is, my lord, and safe in Leicester town,<br>Whither, if it please you, we may now withdraw us. |
| [[King\|KING]] HENRY THE SEVENTH | What men of name are slain on either side? |
| *(didascalia)* | STANLEY(reads) |
| [[King\|KING]] HENRY THE SEVENTH | John Duke of Norfolk, Robert Brackenbury,<br>Walter Lord Ferrers, and Sir William Brandon. |
| [[King\|KING]] HENRY THE SEVENTH | Inter their bodies as becomes their births.<br>Proclaim a pardon to the soldiers fled<br>That in submission will return to us,<br>And then—as we have ta‘en the sacrament—<br>We will unite the white [[Rose\|rose]] and the red.<br>Smile, heaven, upon this fair conjunction,<br>That long have frowned upon their enmity.<br>What traitor hears me and says not ‘Amen’?<br>England hath long been mad, and scarred herself;<br>The brother blindly shed the brother’s [[Blood\|blood]];<br>The father rashly slaughtered his own son;<br>The son, compelled, been butcher to the sire;<br>All that divided York and Lancaster,<br>United in their dire division.<br>O now let Richmond and Elizabeth,<br>The true succeeders of each royal [[house]],<br>By God’s fair ordinance conjoin together,<br>And let their heirs—God, if his will be so—<br>Enrich the [[Time\|time]] to come with smooth-faced peace,<br>With smiling plenty, and fair prosperous days.<br>Abate the edge of traitors, gracious Lord,<br>That would reduce these bloody days again<br>And make poor England weep forth streams of [[Blood\|blood]].<br>Let them not live to taste this land’s increase,<br>That would with treason wound this fair land’s peace.<br>Now civil wounds are stopped; peace lives again.<br>That she may long live here, God say ‘Amen’. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Exeunt |
| ADDITIONAL PASSAGES | The following passages are contained in the Folio text, but not the Quarto; they were apparently omitted from performances.<br>a. AFTER I.2.I54<br>These eyes, which never shed remorseful tear—<br>No, when my father York and Edward wept<br>To hear the piteous moan that Rutland made<br>When black-faced [[Clifford]] shook his [[Sword\|sword]] at him;<br>Nor when thy warlike father like a [[Child\|child]]<br>Told the sad story of my father’s [[Death\|death]]<br>And twenty times made pause to sob and weep,<br>That all the standers-by had wet their cheeks<br>Like trees bedashed with rain. In that sad [[Time\|time]]<br>My manly eyes did scorn an humble tear,<br>And what these sorrows could not thence exhale<br>Thy [[Beauty\|beauty]] hath, and made them blind with weeping.<br>b. AFTER 1.3.166 |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Wert thou not banishèd on [[Pain\|pain]] of [[Death\|death]]? |
| [[Queen Margaret\|QUEEN MARGARET]] | I was, but I do find more [[Pain\|pain]] in banishment<br>Than [[Death\|death]] can yield me here by my abode.<br>c. AFTER I.4.68<br>O God! If my deep prayers cannot appease thee<br>But thou wilt be avenged on my misdeeds,<br>Yet execute thy wrath in me [[Alone\|alone]].<br>O spare my guiltless wife and my poor children.<br>d. AFTER 2.2.88<br>The Folio has Dorset and Rivers enter with [[Queen Elizabeth]] at 2.2.33.I. |
| DORSET | Comfort, dear mother. God is much displeased<br>That you take with unthankfulness his doing.<br>In common worldly things ’tis called ungrateful<br>With dull unwillingness to pay a debt<br>Which with a bounteous hand was kindly lent;<br>Much more to be thus opposite with heaven<br>For it requires the royal debt it lent you. |
| RIVERS | Madam, bethink you like a careful mother<br>Of the young Prince your son. Send straight for him;<br>Let him be crowned. In him your comfort lives.<br>Drown desperate sorrow in dead Edward’s [[Grave\|grave]]<br>And plant your joys in living Edward’s throne.<br>e. AFTER 2.2.II0 |
| RIVERS | Why with some little train, my lord of [[Buckingham]]? |
| [[Buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Marry, my lord, lest by a multitude<br>The new-healed wound of malice should break out,<br>Which would be so much the more dangerous<br>By how much the estate is green and yet ungoverned.<br>Where every [[Horse\|horse]] bears his commanding rein<br>And may direct his course as please himself,<br>As [[Well\|well]] the [[Fear\|fear]] of harm as harm apparent<br>In my opinion ought to be prevented. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I [[Hope\|hope]] the [[King]] made peace with all of us,<br>And the compact is firm and true in me. |
| RIVERS | And so in me, and so I think in all.<br>Yet since it is but green, it should be put<br>To no apparent likelihood of breach,<br>Which haply by much company might be urged.<br>Therefore I say, with noble [[Buckingham]],<br>That it is meet so few should fetch the Prince. |
| [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | And so say I.<br>f. AFTER 3.I.I70<br>And summon him tomorrow to the Tower<br>To sit about the coronation.<br>g. AFTER 3.5.I00<br>Beginning [[Richard]] [[Gloucester]]’s speech. The Folio brings on Lovell and Ratcliffe instead of Catesby at 3.5.19.1. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Go, Lovell, with all [[Speed\|speed]] to Doctor [[Shaw]];<br>(To Ratcliffe) Go thou to Friar Penker. Bid them both<br>Meet me within this hour at Baynard’s [[Castle]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lovell and Ratcliffe, |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | h. AFTER 3.7.I43<br>If not to answer, you might haply think<br>Tongue-tied [[Ambition\|ambition]], not replying, yielded<br>To bear the golden yoke of sovereignty,<br>Which fondly you would here impose on me.<br>If to reprove you for this suit of yours,<br>So seasoned with your faithful [[Love\|love]] to me,<br>Then on the other side I checked my friends.<br>Therefore to speak, and to avoid the first,<br>And then in speaking not to incur the last,<br>Definitively thus I answer you.<br>i. AFTER 4.I.96<br>In the Folio, the characters do not exit during the Duchess of York’s preceding speech. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Stay: yet look back with me unto the Tower.—<br>Pity, you ancient stones, those tender babes,<br>Whom envy hath immured within your walls.<br>Rough cradle for such little pretty ones,<br>Rude ragged [[Nurse\|nurse]], old sullen playfellow<br>For tender princes: use my babies [[Well\|well]].<br>So foolish sorrow bids your stones farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | j. AFTER 4.4.22I |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | You speak as if that I had slain my cousins. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Cousins indeed, and by their uncle cozened<br>Of comfort, kingdom, kindred, [[Freedom\|freedom]], life.<br>Whose hand soever lanced their tender hearts,<br>Thy head all indirectly gave direction.<br>No [[Doubt\|doubt]] the murd‘rous knife was dull and blunt<br>Till it was whetted on thy stone-hard heart<br>To revel in the entrails of my lambs.<br>But that still use of grief makes wild grief tame,<br>My tongue should to thy ears not name my boys<br>Till that my nails were anchored in thine eyes—<br>And I in such a desp’rate bay of [[Death\|death]],<br>Like a poor barque of sails and tackling reft,<br>Rush all to pieces on thy rocky bosom.<br>k. AFTER 4.4.273 |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Say that I did all this for [[Love\|love]] of her. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | Nay, then indeed she cannot choose but hate thee,<br>Having bought [[Love\|love]] with such a bloody spoil. |
| [[King\|KING]] [[Richard\|RICHARD]] | Look what is done cannot be now amended.<br>Men shall deal unadvisedly sometimes,<br>Which after-hours gives leisure to repent.<br>If I did take the kingdom from your sons,<br>To make amends I’ll give it to your daughter.<br>If I have killed the issue of your womb,<br>To quicken your increase I will beget<br>Mine issue of your [[Blood\|blood]] upon your daughter.<br>A grandam’s name is little less in [[Love\|love]]<br>Than is the doting title of a mother.<br>They are as children but one step below,<br>Even of your mettall, of your very [[Blood\|blood]]:<br>Of all one [[Pain\|pain]], save for a [[Night\|night]] of groans<br>Endured of her for whom you bid like sorrow.<br>Your children were vexation to your youth,<br>But mine shall be a comfort to your age.<br>The loss you have is but a son being [[King\|king]],<br>And by that loss your daughter is made [[Queen\|queen]].<br>I cannot make you what amends I would,<br>Therefore accept such kindness as I can.<br>Dorset your son, that with a fearful [[Soul\|soul]]<br>Leads discontented steps in foreign soil,<br>This fair alliance quickly shall call [[Home\|home]]<br>To high promotions and great dignity.<br>The [[King\|king]] that calls your beauteous daughter wife,<br>Familiarly shall call thy Dorset brother.<br>Again shall you be mother to a [[King\|king]],<br>And all the ruins of distressful times<br>Repaired with [[Double\|double]] riches of content.<br>What? We have many goodly days to see.<br>The liquid drops of [[Tears\|tears]] that you have shed<br>Shall come again, transformed to [[Orient\|orient]] pearl,<br>Advantaging their loan with interest<br>Of ten times [[Double\|double]] gain of happiness.<br>Go then, my mother, to thy daughter go.<br>Make bold her bashful years with your experience.<br>Prepare her ears to hear a wooer’s tale.<br>Put in her tender heart th’aspiring flame<br>Of golden sovereignty. Acquaint the Princess<br>With the sweet silent hours of [[Marriage\|marriage]] joys.<br>And when this arm of mine hath chastised<br>The petty [[Rebel\|rebel]], dull-brained [[Buckingham]],<br>Bound with triumphant garlands will I come<br>And lead thy daughter to a conqueror’s bed—<br>To whom I will retail my conquest won,<br>And she shall be sole victoress: Caesar’s Caesar. |
| [[Queen Elizabeth\|QUEEN ELIZABETH]] | What were I best to say? Her father’s brother<br>Would be her lord? Or shall I say her uncle?<br>Or he that slew her brothers and her uncles?<br>Under what title shall I woo for thee,<br>That God, the law, my [[Honour\|honour]], and her [[Love\|love]]<br>Can make seem pleasing to her tender years? |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="Richard III — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,archetype/lover,archetype/shadow,archetype/villain,motif/cross,motif/dreams,motif/home,motif/horse,motif/love,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/soul,motif/summer,motif/time,motif/well,motif/winter,concept/doubt,concept/fear,concept/hope,concept/imprisonment,concept/melancholy,concept/mercy,concept/nature,setting/court,character/clarence,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/gloucester,character/hastings,character/queen,character/richard,character/richard_iii"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Richard]] [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now is the [[Winter\|winter]] of our discontent<br>Made glorious [[Summer\|summer]] by this son of York;<br>And all the clouds that loured upon our [[house]]<br>In the deep bosom of the ocean buried.<br>Now are our brows bound with victorious wreaths,<br>Our bruised arms hung up for monuments,<br>Our stern alarums changed to merry meetings,<br>Our dreadful marches to delightful measures.<br>Grim-visaged war hath smoothed his wrinkled front,<br>And now—instead of mounting barbed steeds<br>To fright the souls of fearful adversaries—<br>He capers nimbly in a lady’s chamber<br>To the lascivious pleasing of a lute.<br>But I, that am not shaped for sportive tricks<br>Nor made to [[Court\|court]] an amorous looking-glass,<br>I that am rudely stamped and want [[Love\|love]]’s majesty<br>To strut before a wanton ambling nymph,<br>I that am curtailed of this fair proportion,<br>Cheated of feature by dissembling [[Nature\|nature]],<br>Deformed, unfinished, sent before my [[Time\|time]]<br>Into this breathing world scarce half made up—<br>And that so lamely and unfashionable<br>That [[dogs]] bark at me as I halt by them—<br>Why, I in this weak piping [[Time\|time]] of peace<br>Have no delight to pass away the [[Time\|time]],<br>Unless to spy my [[Shadow\|shadow]] in the sun<br>And descant on mine own deformity.<br>And therefore since I cannot prove a [[Lover\|lover]]<br>To entertain these fair [[Well\|well]]-spoken days,<br>I am determined to prove a [[Villain\|villain]]<br>And hate the idle pleasures of these days.<br>Plots have I laid, inductions dangerous,<br>By drunken prophecies, libels and [[Dreams\|dreams]]<br>To set my brother [[Clarence]] and the [[King]]<br>In deadly hate the one against the other.<br>And if [[King]] Edward be as true and just<br>As I am subtle false and treacherous,<br>This day should [[Clarence]] closely be mewed up<br>About a prophecy which says that ‘G’<br>Of Edward’s heirs the murderer shall be. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter George Duke of [[Clarence]], guarded, and Sir Robert Brackenbury |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Dive, thoughts, down to my [[Soul\|soul]]: here [[Clarence]] comes.<br>Brother, good day. What means this armèd guard<br>That waits upon your grace? |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | His majesty,<br>Tend’ring my person’s safety, hath appointed<br>This conduct to convey me to the Tower. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Upon what cause? |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Because my name is George. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, my lord, that fault is none of yours.<br>He should for that commit your godfathers.<br>Belike his majesty hath some intent<br>That you should be new-christened in the Tower.<br>But what’s the matter, [[Clarence]]? May I know? |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | Yea, [[Richard]], when I know—for I protest<br>As yet I do not. But as I can learn<br>He hearkens after prophecies and [[Dreams\|dreams]],<br>And from the [[Cross\|cross]]-row plucks the letter ‘G’<br>And says a wizard told him that by ‘G’<br>His issue disinherited should be.<br>And for my name of George begins with ‘G’,<br>It follows in his thought that I am he.<br>These, as I learn, and suchlike toys as these,<br>Hath moved his highness to commit me now. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Why, this it is when men are ruled by women.<br>‘Tis not the [[King]] that sends you to the Tower;<br>My Lady Gray, his wife—[[Clarence]], ’tis she<br>That tempts him to this harsh extremity.<br>Was it not she, and that good man of worship<br>Anthony Woodeville her brother there,<br>That made him send Lord [[Hastings]] to the Tower,<br>From whence this present day he is delivered?<br>We are not safe, [[Clarence]]; we are not safe. |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | By heaven, I think there is no man secure<br>But the [[Queen]]’s kindred, and [[Night\|night]]-walking heralds<br>That trudge betwixt the [[King]] and Mrs Shore.<br>Heard ye not what an humble suppliant<br>Lord [[Hastings]] was for his delivery? |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Humbly complaining to her deity<br>Got my Lord Chamberlain his liberty.<br>I’ll tell you what: I think it is our way,<br>If we will keep in favour with the [[King]],<br>To be her men and wear her livery.<br>The jealous, o’erworn widow and herself,<br>Since that our brother dubbed them gentlewomen,<br>Are mighty gossips in our monarchy. |
| BRACKENBURY | I beseech your graces both to pardon me.<br>His majesty hath straitly given in charge<br>That no man shall have private conference,<br>Of what degree soever, with your brother. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Even so. An’t please your worship, Brackenbury,<br>You may partake of anything we say.<br>We speak no treason, man. We say the [[King]]<br>Is wise and virtuous, and his noble [[Queen]]<br>[[Well]] struck in years, fair, and not jealous.<br>We say that Shore’s wife hath a pretty foot,<br>A cherry lip,<br>A bonny eye, a passing pleasing tongue,<br>And that the [[Queen]]’s kin are made gentlefolks.<br>How say you, sir? Can you deny all this? |
| BRACKENBURY | With this, my lord, myself have naught to do. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Naught to do with Mrs Shore? I tell thee, fellow:<br>He that doth naught with her—excepting one—<br>Were best to do it secretly [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| BRACKENBURY | What one, my lord? |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Her husband, knave. Wouldst thou betray me? |
| BRACKENBURY | I beseech your grace to pardon me, and do withal<br>Forbear your conference with the noble Duke. |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | We know thy charge, Brackenbury, and will obey. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | We are the [[Queen]]’s abjects, and must obey.<br>Brother, farewell. I will unto the [[King]],<br>And whatsoe‘er you will employ me in—<br>Were it to call [[King]] Edward’s widow ‘sister’—<br>I will perform it to enfranchise you.<br>Meantime, this deep disgrace in brotherhood<br>Touches me dearer than you can imagine. |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | I know it pleaseth neither of us [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | [[Well]], your [[Imprisonment\|imprisonment]] shall not be long.<br>I will deliver you or lie for you.<br>Meantime, have patience. |
| [[Clarence\|CLARENCE]] | I must perforce. Farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Clarence]], Brackenbury, and guard, to the Tower |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Go tread the path that thou shalt ne’er return.<br>Simple plain [[Clarence]], I do [[Love\|love]] thee so<br>That I will shortly send thy [[Soul\|soul]] to heaven,<br>If heaven will take the present at our hands.<br>But who comes here? The new-delivered [[Hastings]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord [[Hastings]] from the Tower |
| LORD [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | Good [[Time\|time]] of day unto my gracious lord. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | As much unto my good Lord Chamberlain.<br>[[Well]] are you welcome to the open air.<br>How hath your lordship brooked [[Imprisonment\|imprisonment]]? |
| LORD [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | With patience, noble lord, as prisoners must.<br>But I shall live, my lord, to give them thanks<br>That were the cause of my [[Imprisonment\|imprisonment]]. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No [[Doubt\|doubt]], no [[Doubt\|doubt]]—and so shall [[Clarence]] too,<br>For they that were your enemies are his,<br>And have prevailed as much on him as you. |
| LORD [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | More pity that the eagles should be mewed<br>While kites and buzzards prey at liberty. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What news abroad? 135 |
| LORD [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | No news so bad abroad as this at [[Home\|home]]:<br>The [[King]] is sickly, weak, and [[Melancholy\|melancholy]],<br>And his physicians [[Fear\|fear]] him mightily. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now by Saint Paul, that news is bad indeed.<br>O he hath kept an evil diet long,<br>And overmuch consumed his royal person.<br>’Tis very grievous to be thought upon.<br>Where is he ? In his bed ? |
| LORD [[Hastings\|HASTINGS]] | He is. |
| [[Richard\|RICHARD]] [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Go you before and I will follow you. Exit [[Hastings]]<br><br>He cannot live, I [[Hope\|hope]], and must not die<br>Till George be packed with post-haste up to heaven.<br>I’ll in to urge his hatred more to [[Clarence]],<br>With lies [[Well\|well]] steeled with weighty arguments.<br>And if I fail not in my deep intent,<br>[[Clarence]] hath not another day to live—<br>Which done, God take [[King]] Edward to his [[Mercy\|mercy]]<br>And leave the world for me to bustle in.<br>For then I’ll marry Warwick’s youngest daughter.<br>What though I killed her husband and her father?<br>The readiest way to make the wench amends<br>Is to become her husband and her father,<br>The which will I: not all so much for [[Love\|love]],<br>As for another secret close intent,<br>By marrying her, which I must reach unto.<br>But yet I run before my [[Horse\|horse]] to market.<br>[[Clarence]] still breathes, Edward still lives and reigns;<br>When they are gone, then must I count my gains. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
